  &#x20;His spokesperson, [Stéphane Dujarric](10302459.md) expressed concern over reports of [UN-marked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_the_United_Nations) vehicles being used during the protests and stated that such vehicles should only be used for official [UN peacekeeping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_peacekeeping) or political missions, adding that he had raised the issue to the Bangladeshi government.

  [^cite_note-United_Nations-2024-409]

  &#x20;[United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) [Volker Türk](71721726.md) urged the government to engage with student protesters and to stop the violence.

  [^cite_note-431]

-  [Germany](11867.md): A [foreign ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Foreign_Office) spokesperson emphasises that it is important that Bangladesh remain democratic amid the unrest in the country.

  [^cite_note-432]

-  [European Union](9317.md): Ambassador to Bangladesh Charles Whiteley stated that he "wants to see a quick resolution of present situation" and urges the government for "avoidance of further violence and bloodshed".

  [^cite_note-433]

  [^cite_note-434]

  &#x20;[EU Foreign Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Foreign_and_Security_Policy) chief [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) condemned the "excessive use of force" against protesters and called for accountability and respect for human rights. He emphasized the need for an investigation into the deaths, arrests, and property damage.

  [^cite_note-435]

### Organisations

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) urged the Bangladeshi government to "end the crisis, punish those committing serious crimes, and protect" protesting students. They also called for increased scrutiny of Bangladeshi security forces by the UK and the EU.[^cite_note-436]

[UNICEF](19698110.md) expressed concerns over the death of 32 children in the protests. Sanjay Wijesekera, UNICEF Regional Director for South Asia, urged swift measures to ensure that children can return to school.[^cite_note-unicef-306]

The [All India Students Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Students_Association) denounced the violence and expressed their solidarity with student protesters.[^cite_note-437] The [Awami Ittehad Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sindh_Awami_Ittehad) also condemned the violence and urged safety for the Indian nationals residing there.[^cite_note-438]

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) condemned the attacks against protesters and called on the government "to immediately guarantee the safety of all peaceful protesters and proper treatment of all those injured".[^cite_note-441]

[Ma Thida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma_Thida), Chair of [PEN International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEN_International)'s [Writers in Prison Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEN_International#Writers_in_Prison_Committee), condemned the violence and called on the Bangladesh government to protect journalists and ensure their freedom of expression.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443]

[International Federation for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_for_Human_Rights) and [Odhikar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odhikar) urged the government to investigate the law enforcement crackdown and hold officers accountable for unnecessary or disproportionate force. They also called for an immediate, independent investigation into attacks on protesters by [BCL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Chhatra_League) members.[^cite_note-444]

Célia Mercier, Head of [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md)'s South Asia Desk, demanded that those responsible for the violent attacks on journalists be identified and brought to justice immediately. She also called on authorities to guarantee the safety of journalists and the right to information.[^cite_note-445]

The digital civil rights NGO [Access Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_Now) urged the government for unrestricted internet access and transparency during disruptions, with service providers giving prior notification and explanations to users.[^cite_note-446]

[^cite_note-OHCHR-343]: &#x20;[Preliminary Analysis of Recent Protests and Unrest in Bangladesh](https://www.ohchr.org/en/documents/country-reports/preliminary-analysis-recent-protests-and-unrest-bangladesh) (Report). [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md). 16 August 2024. pp. 6–7. According to available public reports by media and the protest movement itself, between 16 July and 11 August, more than 600 people were killed. Of these, nearly 400 deaths were reported from 16 July to 4 August, while around 250 people were reportedly killed following the new wave of protests between 5 and 6 August. \[...] The reported death toll is likely an underestimate, as information collection has been hindered by restrictions on movement due to the curfew and the internet shutdown. Furthermore, hospitals were reportedly prevented by State authorities from providing details of those killed and injured. The majority of deaths and injuries have been attributed to the security forces and the student wing affiliated with the Awami League.
